    "id": "Mo memiliki sejarah sebagai nama panda sekaligus penggambaran makhluk campuran dalam tradisi Tiongkok.",
    "en": "Mo has a history as a name for the panda and as a composite creature depicted in Chinese tradition.",

    {
      "id": "Mo memiliki sejarah sebagai nama panda sekaligus penggambaran makhluk campuran dalam tradisi Tiongkok. Dalam uraian Bai Juyi, Mo menggabungkan belalai gajah, mata badak, ekor sapi, dan kaki harimau.",
      "en": "Mo has a history as a name for the panda and as a composite creature depicted in Chinese tradition. Bai Juyi describes Mo with an elephant trunk, rhinoceros eyes, a cow tail, and tiger paws.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mo-chinese-zoology-c01",
        "mo-chinese-zoology-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bai Juyi mengaitkan gambar Mo dengan penolakan pengaruh jahat dan kulitnya dengan perlindungan dari wabah. Baku Jepang mengembangkan motif gambar pelindung kesehatan menjadi makhluk pemakan mimpi buruk. Uraian pemakan logam berasal dari komentar Guo Pu, bukan langsung dari teks awal Shan Hai Jing. Hingga akhir Qing, nama Mo masih mencakup panda dan makhluk campuran, sebelum dikaitkan dengan tapir melalui kekeliruan identifikasi.",
      "en": "Bai Juyi associates images of Mo with warding off evil and its pelt with protection from contagion. The Japanese baku develops the protective image motif into dream devouring to prevent nightmares.       "source_id": "mo-chinese-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "Mo (Chinese: 貘) was the Chinese name for the giant panda from the 3rd century BCE to the 19th century CE. ... Chinese woodblocks depict the mò (貘) as having an elephant trunk, rhinoceros eyes, cow tail and tiger paws, following the description of 9th-century Tang poet Bai/Bo Juyi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mo memiliki sejarah sebagai nama panda sekaligus penggambaran makhluk campuran dalam tradisi Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Mo has a history as a name for the panda and as a composite creature depicted in Chinese tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-chinese-zoology-c02",
      "source_id": "mo-chinese-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "The mò has elephant trunk, rhinoceros eyes, cow tail, and tiger paws [貘者象鼻犀目牛尾虎足]. It inhabits the mountains and valleys of the south. To sleep on its pelt repels contagion [寢其皮辟瘟]. To draw its form repels evil [圖其形辟邪]. In the past I suffered from wind-ailment of the head [病頭風], and whenever I slept I always protected my head with a small screen.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 45",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam uraian Bai Juyi, Mo menggabungkan belalai gajah, mata badak, ekor sapi, dan kaki harimau.",
        "en": "Bai Juyi describes Mo with an elephant trunk, rhinoceros eyes, a cow tail, and tiger paws."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-chinese-zoology-c03",
      "source_id": "mo-chinese-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "The mò has elephant trunk, rhinoceros eyes, cow tail, and tiger paws [貘者象鼻犀目牛尾虎足]. It inhabits the mountains and valleys of the south. To sleep on its pelt repels contagion [寢其皮辟瘟]. To draw its form repels evil [圖其形辟邪]. In the past I suffered from wind-ailment of the head [病頭風], and whenever I slept I always protected my head with a small screen.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 45",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bai Juyi mengaitkan gambar Mo dengan penolakan pengaruh jahat dan kulitnya dengan perlindungan dari wabah.",
        "en": "Bai Juyi associates images of Mo with warding off evil and its pelt with protection from contagion."

    },
    {
      "id": "mo-chinese-zoology-c04",
      "source_id": "mo-chinese-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "The Japanese baku (獏) changed the Chinese myth about the mò image preventing illness to dream-devouring in order to prevent nightmares.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 46",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Baku Jepang mengembangkan motif gambar pelindung kesehatan menjadi makhluk pemakan mimpi buruk.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-chinese-zoology-c05",
      "source_id": "mo-chinese-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "Bai's \"eats iron and copper, and eats nothing else\" reference comes from Guo Pu's 4th century commentary to the Shanhai jing and not the pre-Han classic text. The Shanhai jing proper mentions the mengbao \"ferocious leopard\" on South Mountain, which Guo notes as a metal-eating beast similar to the mò, and mentions Lai Mountain, which he glosses as a mò habitat.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 47",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian pemakan logam berasal dari komentar Guo Pu, bukan langsung dari teks awal Shan Hai Jing.",
        "en": "The metal eating description comes from Guo Pu's commentary rather than the early Shan Hai Jing text itself."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-chinese-zoology-c06",
      "source_id": "mo-chinese-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "Up until the late Qing dynasty (1644–1912), the Chinese name mo (貘) continued referring to both \"giant panda\" and \"chimera with an elephant trunk, rhinoceros eyes, cow tail, and tiger paws\", and owing to Jean-Pierre Abel-Rémusat's mix-up in the 1820s, mò was misidentified as the recently discovered \"Malayan tapir\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 51",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hingga akhir Qing, nama Mo masih mencakup panda dan makhluk campuran, sebelum dikaitkan dengan tapir melalui kekeliruan identifikasi.",

      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi awal yang bertahan dalam Taiping Guangji menyebut dua belas telinga.",

    {
      "id": "nine-eared-hound-c03",
      "source_id": "nine-eared-hound-s1",
      "quote": "One version of the myth is found in Chapter 12 of Yuan Mei's What the Master Would Not Discuss (1788). The Nine-Eared Hound belonged to a hunter surnamed Chen (陳).       "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anjing milik pemburu bermarga Chen ini meramalkan jumlah buruan melalui telinganya yang bergerak.",
        "en": "The dog belongs to a hunter named Chen and predicts the number of prey through its quivering ears."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nine-eared-hound-c05",
      "source_id": "nine-eared-hound-s1",
      "quote": "Chen found a huge egg and brought it home. The next day, during a thunderstorm, the egg cracked open, revealing a lovely baby.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anjing tersebut membawa Chen kepada telur besar yang kemudian menetas menjadi bayi ketika terjadi badai.",
        "en": "The dog leads Chen to a large egg that later opens during a thunderstorm to reveal a baby."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nine-eared-hound-c06",
      "source_id": "nine-eared-hound-s1",
      "quote": "In another version, the baby had the characters \"Leizhou\" written in one palm and the characters \"Chen Wenyu\" written in the other, and Chen Wenyu became his name.         "scorpion-demoness-c02"
      ]

    {

Her true form is a giant scorpion as large as a pipa. In the narrative, the Scorpion Demoness stands out as the sole character capable of harming both the Buddha and Sun Wukong.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sengatan ekornya mampu melukai Buddha dan Sun Wukong dalam cerita.",
        "en": "Her tail sting can injure both the Buddha and Sun Wukong in the story."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scorpion-demoness-c04",
      "source_id": "scorpion-demoness-s1",
      "quote": "The Scorpion Demoness resides in Pipa Cave (琵琶洞) on Venom Mountain (毒敵山), near the Women's Kingdom. When Tang Sanzang and his disciples are about to leave the Women's Kingdom, the scorpion demoness suddenly appears, abducting Sanzang to her cave. There, she attempts to seduce and persuade him into marriage, leading him into the bridal suite she has prepared.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menghuni Pipa Cave di Venom Mountain, dekat Women's Kingdom.",
        "en": "She lives in Pipa Cave on Venom Mountain near the Women's Kingdom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scorpion-demoness-c05",
      "source_id": "scorpion-demoness-s1",
      "quote": "The Scorpion Demoness resides in Pipa Cave (琵琶洞) on Venom Mountain (毒敵山), near the Women's Kingdom. When Tang Sanzang and his disciples are about to leave the Women's Kingdom, the scorpion demoness suddenly appears, abducting Sanzang to her cave. There, she attempts to seduce and persuade him into marriage, leading him into the bridal suite she has prepared.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",

    {

      "en": "Shuzi, also called Shūji, is an ancient Chinese legendary animal resembling a large tiger. It is described with a very large body and a long tail.",

        "shuzi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia umumnya tidak memburu hewan hidup, tetapi menyerang harimau dan macan tutul. Versi cerita berbeda mengenai apakah hasil buruannya dimakan.     {
      "id": "shuzi-c01",
      "source_id": "shuzi-s1",
      "quote": "酋耳（しゅうじ）とは古代中国の伝説上の生き物。大きな虎のようなすがたをしているとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shuzi atau Shūji adalah hewan legendaris Tiongkok kuno menyerupai harimau besar.",
        "en": "Shuzi, also called Shūji, is an ancient Chinese legendary animal resembling a large tiger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shuzi-c02",
      "source_id": "shuzi-s1",
      "quote": "見た目は虎のようで体はとても大きく尾もとても長いが、決して生きた獣を捕食しないという。ただし、虎や豹を目にすると態度は変わり必ず襲ってそれを殺すと伝えられていた。狩り捕った虎や豹は食べるとされる場合もあるが、酋耳は狩るのみでその肉を食べたりはしないとも語られる。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tubuh dan ekornya digambarkan sangat besar serta panjang.",
        "en": "It is described with a very large body and a long tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shuzi-c03",
      "source_id": "shuzi-s1",
      "quote": "見た目は虎のようで体はとても大きく尾もとても長いが、決して生きた獣を捕食しないという。ただし、虎や豹を目にすると態度は変わり必ず襲ってそれを殺すと伝えられていた。狩り捕った虎や豹は食べるとされる場合もあるが、酋耳は狩るのみでその肉を食べたりはしないとも語られる。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia umumnya tidak memburu hewan hidup, tetapi menyerang harimau dan macan tutul.",
        "en": "It generally avoids hunting living animals but attacks tigers and leopards."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shuzi-c04",
      "source_id": "shuzi-s1",
      "quote": "見た目は虎のようで体はとても大きく尾もとても長いが、決して生きた獣を捕食しないという。ただし、虎や豹を目にすると態度は変わり必ず襲ってそれを殺すと伝えられていた。狩り捕った虎や豹は食べるとされる場合もあるが、酋耳は狩るのみでその肉を食べたりはしないとも語られる。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi cerita berbeda mengenai apakah hasil buruannya dimakan.",
        "en": "Versions differ over whether it eats the animals it kills."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shuzi-c05",
      "source_id": "shuzi-s1",
      "quote": "『三才図会』では、王者の威勢が四夷に及んだ際に世に出現する獣であるとされている。『逸周書』「王会」の解では各地から贈られて来ためずらしい禽獣魚介のうちのひとつとして贈られていることが記されているが、これを献上している央林（『三才図会』では「英林山」とされている）については西の方角に属する地である以外にはあまり詳しく分かっていない。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sancai Tuhui menghubungkan kemunculannya dengan kewibawaan penguasa yang meluas ke wilayah luar.",
        "en": "The Sancai Tuhui connects its appearance with a ruler's authority extending to the surrounding peoples."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shuzi-c06",
      "source_id": "shuzi-s1",
      "quote": "同様に獣を捕食しないとされる中国に伝わる霊獣には騶虞（すうぐ）というものもあり、そちらは仁獣とされている。性質が似通っていることから同じ霊獣と見られたり、混同が行われて来ており「虎や豹を見ると狩るが、食べない」と言われたりする点や「長い尾が特徴である」とされる点など互いの属性がまじりつつ伝わっている面もある。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shuzi kadang disamakan dengan Zouyu sehingga ciri kedua makhluk saling bercampur.",

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-s1",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-c01",
      "source_id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "The Nine-tailed turtle (traditional Chinese: 九尾龜; simplified Chinese: 九尾龟) is a legendary Chinese turtle.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kura-kura Berekor Sembilan adalah hewan legendaris Tiongkok.",
        "en": "The Nine-tailed Turtle is a legendary animal from China."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-c02",
      "source_id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "In an appended supplement to Records of the Grand Historian, the Han dynasty scholar Chu Shaosun (104?–30 BC) mentioned eight famous turtles particularly suitable for plastromancy or turtle shell divination, one being called the \"Turtle of Nine Provinces\" (九州龜).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Turtle of Nine Provinces muncul dalam tambahan Chu Shaosun pada Records of the Grand Historian.",
        "en": "The name Turtle of Nine Provinces appears in Chu Shaosun's supplement to the Records of the Grand Historian."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-c03",
      "source_id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "Over time \"Turtle of Nine Provinces\" became \"Turtle of Nine Tails\" in folklore. According to one source, it takes a turtle three thousand years to grow another tail.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat, Turtle of Nine Provinces berkembang menjadi Turtle of Nine Tails.",
        "en": "In folklore, Turtle of Nine Provinces develops into Turtle of Nine Tails."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-c04",
      "source_id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "Two extant stories from imperial China both appear to advocate for the life release of nine-tailed turtles.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dua kisah dari Tiongkok kekaisaran dipahami sebagai dukungan terhadap pelepasan kura-kura hidup.",
        "en": "Two stories from imperial China are interpreted as encouraging the release of living turtles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-c05",
      "source_id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "According to a story compiled by Hong Mai (1123–1202) in his Yijian Zhi, during the Jin–Song Wars of 1126, when the Song \"southern capital\" Yingtian Prefecture (modern Shangqiu) was under siege, a huge nine-tailed turtle appeared in the city. It was described as being three chi tall, \"as big as a wheel\", with nine bony tails and a yellow, waxy carapace.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yijian Zhi karya Hong Mai memuat kisah kura-kura yang muncul ketika Yingtian dikepung pada 1126.",
        "en": "Hong Mai's Yijian Zhi includes a turtle appearing during the siege of Yingtian in 1126."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-c06",
      "source_id": "the-nine-tailed-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "According to a story compiled by Hong Mai (1123–1202) in his Yijian Zhi, during the Jin–Song Wars of 1126, when the Song \"southern capital\" Yingtian Prefecture (modern Shangqiu) was under siege, a huge nine-tailed turtle appeared in the city. It was described as being three chi tall, \"as big as a wheel\", with nine bony tails and a yellow, waxy carapace.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kura-kura dalam kisah itu sebesar roda, dengan sembilan ekor bertulang dan karapas kuning seperti lilin.",
        "en": "The turtle in that story is wheel sized, with nine bony tails and a yellow waxy carapace."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kura-kura dan labi-labi berkaki tiga merupakan hewan mitos dari Tiongkok.",
    "en": "Three-legged turtles and softshell turtles are mythical animals from China.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "three-legged-turtle-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kura-kura dan labi-labi berkaki tiga merupakan hewan mitos dari Tiongkok. Erya memberi nama Fen untuk kura-kura bercangkang keras dan Nai untuk labi-labi.",
      "en": "Three-legged turtles and softshell turtles are mythical animals from China. The Erya names the hard shelled turtle Fen and the softshell turtle Nai.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "three-legged-turtle-c01",
        "three-legged-turtle-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Terjemahan Shan Hai Jing menyebut kura-kura berkaki tiga di River Mad pada Mount Bigbitter. Kisah lain menempatkan labi-labi berkaki tiga dengan ekor bercabang di River Trailer.",
      "en": "A translation of the Shan Hai Jing places three-footed turtles in River Mad on Mount Bigbitter. Another passage places three-footed softshell turtles with branching tails in River Trailer.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "three-legged-turtle-c03",
        "three-legged-turtle-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

      "id": "three-legged-turtle-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three-legged_turtle",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "three-legged-turtle-c01",
      "source_id": "three-legged-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "Three-legged turtle (simplified Chinese: 三足龟; traditional Chinese: 三足龜; pinyin: Sānzúguī) and Three-legged softshell turtle (simplified Chinese: 三足鳖; traditional Chinese: 三足鱉; pinyin: Sānzúbiē) are mythical turtles from China.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kura-kura dan labi-labi berkaki tiga merupakan hewan mitos dari Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Three-legged turtles and softshell turtles are mythical animals from China."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "three-legged-turtle-c02",
      "source_id": "three-legged-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "According to the ancient dictionary Erya, the former (pertaining to hardshell turtles including tortoises) is properly called Fen (simplified Chinese: 贲; traditional Chinese: 賁; pinyin: Fén), and the latter, Nai (Chinese: 能; pinyin: Nái).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Erya memberi nama Fen untuk kura-kura bercangkang keras dan Nai untuk labi-labi.",
        "en": "The Erya names the hard shelled turtle Fen and the softshell turtle Nai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "three-legged-turtle-c03",
      "source_id": "three-legged-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "...On the south face of Mount Bigbitter the River Mad rises and flows southwest to empty into the River Person. Three-footed turtles are numerous in the River Mad. Those who eat it won't have a serious illness, and it can be taken to cure a swelling.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Shan Hai Jing menyebut kura-kura berkaki tiga di River Mad pada Mount Bigbitter.",
        "en": "A translation of the Shan Hai Jing places three-footed turtles in River Mad on Mount Bigbitter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "three-legged-turtle-c04",
      "source_id": "three-legged-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "...The River Trailer rises on its summit and runs a hidden course to its base. This river contains many three-footed [softshell] turtles with a branching tail. If you eat it, you won't succumb to the plague of the malign force.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah lain menempatkan labi-labi berkaki tiga dengan ekor bercabang di River Trailer.",
        "en": "Another passage places three-footed softshell turtles with branching tails in River Trailer."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "Lili is a piglike legendary animal in Chinese tradition. Its feet have projections compared to the rear spur of a chicken's foot.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10409490-c01",
        "wd-q10409490-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Suaranya menyerupai gonggongan anjing. Kemunculannya dikaitkan dengan kegiatan pembangunan di suatu tempat. Bagian selatan kedua Shan Hai Jing menempatkan Lili pada Gunung Gui. Teks klasik menamai hewan bertaji dan bersuara anjing ini Lili.",
      "en": "Its voice resembles a dog's bark. Its appearance is associated with construction work in a locality. The second southern section of the Shan Hai Jing places Lili on Mount Gui. The classical text names this spurred animal with a doglike voice Lili.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Luoluo adalah burung pemakan manusia yang tercatat dalam Shan Hai Jing.",
    "en": "Luoluo is a human eating bird recorded in the Shan Hai Jing.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q10478981-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Luoluo adalah burung pemakan manusia yang tercatat dalam Shan Hai Jing. Kisah burung Luoluo menempatkannya pada Gunung Lai.",
      "en": "Luoluo is a human eating bird recorded in the Shan Hai Jing. The account of the Luoluo bird places it on Mount Lai.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10478981-c01",
        "wd-q10478981-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Luoluo juga dipakai untuk hewan buas kebiruan mirip harimau dalam bagian lain teks. Guo Pu menyatakan bahwa ciri burung Luoluo tidak dijelaskan secara rinci. Teks klasik menyebut pepohonan cendana dan murbei di Gunung Lai, tempat banyak Luoluo.",
      "en": "The name Luoluo is also used for a bluish tigerlike beast elsewhere in the text. Guo Pu notes that the Luoluo bird's characteristics are not explained in detail. The classical text mentions sandalwood and mulberry trees on Mount Lai, where Luoluo are numerous.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10478981-c03",
        "wd-q10478981-c04",
        "wd-q10478981-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

      "title": "山海經/西山經 - 维基文库，自由的图书馆",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikisource",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10478981-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10478981-s1",
      "quote": "罗罗是中國古代传说中的吃人鸟，記載於《山海经·山经·卷二·莱山》中。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Luoluo adalah burung pemakan manusia yang tercatat dalam Shan Hai Jing.",
        "en": "Luoluo is a human eating bird recorded in the Shan Hai Jing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10478981-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10478981-s1",
      "quote": "於中国先秦古籍《山海经》載：「又西三百五十里，曰萊山，其木多檀楮，其鳥多羅羅，是食人。」",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah burung Luoluo menempatkannya pada Gunung Lai.",
        "en": "The account of the Luoluo bird places it on Mount Lai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10478981-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10478981-s1",
      "quote": "罗罗也於《山海经·海外北经》載是一種青色似虎的猛兽，如郝懿行說：「海外北經有青獸，狀如虎，名曰羅羅，此鳥與之同名。」罗罗或就是形容這種鳥似虎凶猛，但《山海经》中未有詳述，如郭璞說：「羅羅之鳥，所未詳也。」",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Luoluo juga dipakai untuk hewan buas kebiruan mirip harimau dalam bagian lain teks.",
        "en": "The name Luoluo is also used for a bluish tigerlike beast elsewhere in the text."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10478981-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10478981-s1",
      "quote": "罗罗也於《山海经·海外北经》載是一種青色似虎的猛兽，如郝懿行說：「海外北經有青獸，狀如虎，名曰羅羅，此鳥與之同名。」罗罗或就是形容這種鳥似虎凶猛，但《山海经》中未有詳述，如郭璞說：「羅羅之鳥，所未詳也。」",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guo Pu menyatakan bahwa ciri burung Luoluo tidak dijelaskan secara rinci.",
        "en": "Guo Pu notes that the Luoluo bird's characteristics are not explained in detail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10478981-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q10478981-s2",
      "quote": "又西三百裏五十里，曰萊山，其木多檀楮，其鳥多羅羅〈羅羅之鳥所未詳也。〉，是食人。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 36",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks klasik menyebut pepohonan cendana dan murbei di Gunung Lai, tempat banyak Luoluo.",
        "en": "The classical text mentions sandalwood and mulberry trees on Mount Lai, where Luoluo are numerous."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Suanyu adalah burung dalam Shan Hai Jing yang memiliki rupa menyerupai ular. Makhluk ini mempunyai empat sayap.",
      "en": "Suanyu is a bird in the Shan Hai Jing with a snakelike appearance. The creature has four wings.",

        "wd-q10563786-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia juga memiliki enam mata dan tiga kaki. Bunyinya dipahami sebagai seruan namanya sendiri. Kemunculannya dianggap pertanda bahwa suatu tempat akan mengalami ketakutan. Teks klasik menempatkan Suanyu pada Gunung Jing.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tulou, juga disebut Lou, adalah hewan buas dalam mitologi Tiongkok. Tubuhnya menyerupai domba dengan empat tanduk.",
      "en": "Tulou, also called Lou, is a beast in Chinese mythology. Its body resembles a sheep with four horns.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10927868-c01",
        "wd-q10927868-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tulou digambarkan memangsa manusia. Shan Hai Jing menempatkannya di Kunlun, tempat kediaman penguasa langit di dunia bawah. Tulou juga disebut dalam catatan Bamboo Annals dan Wei Gongqing Shang Zunhao Zou. Dalam bagian Kunlun itu, Lu Wu disebut mengawasi kediaman penguasa langit.",
      "en": "Tulou is described as eating humans. The Shan Hai Jing places it on Kunlun, the heavenly ruler's dwelling in the lower world. Tulou is also mentioned in the Bamboo Annals commentary and Wei Gongqing Shang Zunhao Zou. In that Kunlun passage, Lu Wu is named as overseeing the heavenly ruler's dwelling.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10927868-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10927868-s1",
      "quote": "土螻者，乃古譚之異獸焉，據《山海經》所載，其狀如羊而四角，是食人。居於西南四百里，曰昆侖之丘，是實惟帝之下都，神陸吾司之。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tulou, juga disebut Lou, adalah hewan buas dalam mitologi Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Tulou, also called Lou, is a beast in Chinese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10927868-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10927868-s2",
      "quote": "最初出现于《山海經》中，文中提到昆仑山是天帝在下界的都邑，土蝼就生活在这里。它的形状像羊，却长着四只角，这种凶恶的野兽见人就吃。还有一种记载，把“蝼”字写作“褛”，说它的四只角非常尖锐，触到什么东西，那就难以活命。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tubuhnya menyerupai domba dengan empat tanduk.",
        "en": "Its body resembles a sheep with four horns."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10927868-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10927868-s2",
      "quote": "最初出现于《山海經》中，文中提到昆仑山是天帝在下界的都邑，土蝼就生活在这里。它的形状像羊，却长着四只角，这种凶恶的野兽见人就吃。还有一种记载，把“蝼”字写作“褛”，说它的四只角非常尖锐，触到什么东西，那就难以活命。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tulou digambarkan memangsa manusia.",
        "en": "Tulou is described as eating humans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10927868-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10927868-s2",
      "quote": "最初出现于《山海經》中，文中提到昆仑山是天帝在下界的都邑，土蝼就生活在这里。它的形状像羊，却长着四只角，这种凶恶的野兽见人就吃。还有一种记载，把“蝼”字写作“褛”，说它的四只角非常尖锐，触到什么东西，那就难以活命。",

      "context": "historical-record",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Huoxing adalah roh Mars yang digambarkan sebagai anak aneh dalam legenda Tiongkok.",
    "en": "Huoxing is the spirit of Mars depicted as an uncanny child in Chinese legend.",

    {
      "id": "Huoxing adalah roh Mars yang digambarkan sebagai anak aneh dalam legenda Tiongkok. Kisah itu dikaitkan dengan Soushen Ji karya Gan Bao.",
      "en": "Huoxing is the spirit of Mars depicted as an uncanny child in Chinese legend. The account is associated with Gan Bao's Soushen Ji.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q15925221-c01",
        "wd-q15925221-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah bertanggal 260 menggambarkan anak berusia sekitar enam atau tujuh tahun dengan pakaian hijau. Matanya memancarkan cahaya yang menakutkan anak-anak lainnya. Anak itu mengaku sebagai Mars dan meramalkan bahwa Tiga Kerajaan akan dikuasai keluarga Sima. Ketika orang dewasa datang, ia terbang ke langit dan berubah menjadi kain putih berekor panjang.",
      "en": "The account dated to 260 describes a child about six or seven years old wearing green. His eyes emit light that frightens the other children. The child identifies himself as Mars and predicts that the Three Kingdoms will belong to the Sima family. When adults arrive, he rises into the sky and becomes a white cloth with a long trailing tail.",

        "wd-q15925221-c04",

      "title": "熒惑星 (妖怪) - 维基百科，自由的百科全书",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "zh",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-01"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q15925221-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q15925221-s1",
      "quote": "熒惑星，傳說在三国时期吴国建国之初出现，预言三国终将归司马家所有的怪童。在古人的观念中,谣殲是荧惑星之精降临变成童子而为。荧惑则是文言文指火星的意思。所以水木茂的《中国妖怪事典》将其译为“火星”。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huoxing adalah roh Mars yang digambarkan sebagai anak aneh dalam legenda Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Huoxing is the spirit of Mars depicted as an uncanny child in Chinese legend."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q15925221-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q15925221-s1",
      "quote": "根據中国晋代干宝的《搜神记》，大约在吳國建國之初，鎮守邊境的守備隊將士，其妻兒需作為人質被強行押置在京城。這些孩子總是成群結伙地一起嬉戲玩耍。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu dikaitkan dengan Soushen Ji karya Gan Bao.",
        "en": "The account is associated with Gan Bao's Soushen Ji."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q15925221-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q15925221-s1",
      "quote": "永安三年（260年）二月某日，孩子如常玩耍時，突然出現一個陌生的小朋友，年紀大约六、七歲。身高四尺多，身穿綠衣。孩子都覺得很奇怪，七嘴八舌地問：「這個傢伙從沒看過耶，你是哪家的小孩？」這個小孩回答：「我看你們玩得很高興，就過來啦！」大家定神一瞧，只見這個小孩兩眼閃爍著強烈光芒，好像要射殺人一般。孩子們感到非常害怕，又再問：「你從那里來的？」他回答：「你們怕我嗎？我不是人，是熒惑星（火星）。其實，我有事要告訴你們；三國終將歸司馬氏。」此話一出，孩子們都嚇破膽，其中一人急忙跑去叫大人來。大人一看到這個小孩，那孩子就说：「各位，再會了！」隨之便蜷曲身體飛向天際。孩子的身體瞬間變成了一塊白布，緩緩地朝天空飛去。大家仰頭望去，只見白布拖著長長的尾巴輕輕地飄走，不一會兒就消失不見。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah bertanggal 260 menggambarkan anak berusia sekitar enam atau tujuh tahun dengan pakaian hijau.",
        "en": "The account dated to 260 describes a child about six or seven years old wearing green."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q15925221-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q15925221-s1",
      "quote": "永安三年（260年）二月某日，孩子如常玩耍時，突然出現一個陌生的小朋友，年紀大约六、七歲。身高四尺多，身穿綠衣。孩子都覺得很奇怪，七嘴八舌地問：「這個傢伙從沒看過耶，你是哪家的小孩？」這個小孩回答：「我看你們玩得很高興，就過來啦！」大家定神一瞧，只見這個小孩兩眼閃爍著強烈光芒，好像要射殺人一般。孩子們感到非常害怕，又再問：「你從那里來的？」他回答：「你們怕我嗎？我不是人，是熒惑星（火星）。其實，我有事要告訴你們；三國終將歸司馬氏。」此話一出，孩子們都嚇破膽，其中一人急忙跑去叫大人來。大人一看到這個小孩，那孩子就说：「各位，再會了！」隨之便蜷曲身體飛向天際。孩子的身體瞬間變成了一塊白布，緩緩地朝天空飛去。大家仰頭望去，只見白布拖著長長的尾巴輕輕地飄走，不一會兒就消失不見。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Matanya memancarkan cahaya yang menakutkan anak-anak lainnya.",
        "en": "His eyes emit light that frightens the other children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q15925221-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q15925221-s1",
      "quote": "他回答：「你們怕我嗎？我不是人，是熒惑星（火星）。其實，我有事要告訴你們；三國終將歸司馬氏。」",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anak itu mengaku sebagai Mars dan meramalkan bahwa Tiga Kerajaan akan dikuasai keluarga Sima.",
        "en": "The child identifies himself as Mars and predicts that the Three Kingdoms will belong to the Sima family."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q15925221-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q15925221-s1",
      "quote": "大人一看到這個小孩，那孩子就说：「各位，再會了！」隨之便蜷曲身體飛向天際。孩子的身體瞬間變成了一塊白布，緩緩地朝天空飛去。大家仰頭望去，只見白布拖著長長的尾巴輕輕地飄走，不一會兒就消失不見。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika orang dewasa datang, ia terbang ke langit dan berubah menjadi kain putih berekor panjang.",
        "en": "When adults arrive, he rises into the sky and becomes a white cloth with a long trailing tail."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

      "id": "wd-q17060695-s1",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q17060695-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q17060695-s1",
      "quote": "螺女，又称海螺姑娘、田螺姑娘，是中国一些地区民间的传说中的一个仙女。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gadis Siput adalah peri dalam cerita rakyat di sejumlah wilayah Tiongkok.",
        "en": "The Snail Girl is a fairy in folk narratives from several parts of China."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17060695-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17060695-s1",
      "quote": "根据东晋陶渊明的《搜神后记》记载，在晋安帝年间，侯官（今属福州市闽侯县）人谢端少年失怙，由邻居养大。谢端恭谨守法、勤勤勉勉，但是年龄很大了还没有娶妻，邻居都十分着急，为他寻找，都没有成功。一天，谢端得到了一个七色海螺，其大如斗，觉得这个海螺很稀奇，不忍心把它吃掉，就将那个海螺养在水缸里。此后，谢端每天出门回来，都會看到滿桌佳肴。谢端以为是邻居帮他煮饭，前去感谢。邻居感到莫名其妙，说是他妻子做的饭。谢端心里觉得奇怪，有一天他假装出门，躲在門缝後，看見水缸裡冒出来一個漂亮女子，為他煮好飯菜並把家裡打掃乾淨。谢端很奇怪，前去水缸前查看，只看见女子没有看见田螺，便询问女子是谁。女子走避不及，只得回答称自己是天界的白水素女，天帝派来给谢端煮饭充当家庭主妇，待谢端十年后致富了便离去；现在原型被瞥见，只得马上离去。谢端再三挽留不成。忽然天上刮风下雨，女子随风而去。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel menempatkan kisah Xie Duan dalam Soushen Houji yang dikaitkan dengan Tao Yuanming.",
        "en": "The article places the Xie Duan narrative in Soushen Houji, attributed to Tao Yuanming."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17060695-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17060695-s1",
      "quote": "根据东晋陶渊明的《搜神后记》记载，在晋安帝年间，侯官（今属福州市闽侯县）人谢端少年失怙，由邻居养大。谢端恭谨守法、勤勤勉勉，但是年龄很大了还没有娶妻，邻居都十分着急，为他寻找，都没有成功。一天，谢端得到了一个七色海螺，其大如斗，觉得这个海螺很稀奇，不忍心把它吃掉，就将那个海螺养在水缸里。此后，谢端每天出门回来，都會看到滿桌佳肴。谢端以为是邻居帮他煮饭，前去感谢。邻居感到莫名其妙，说是他妻子做的饭。谢端心里觉得奇怪，有一天他假装出门，躲在門缝後，看見水缸裡冒出来一個漂亮女子，為他煮好飯菜並把家裡打掃乾淨。谢端很奇怪，前去水缸前查看，只看见女子没有看见田螺，便询问女子是谁。女子走避不及，只得回答称自己是天界的白水素女，天帝派来给谢端煮饭充当家庭主妇，待谢端十年后致富了便离去；现在原型被瞥见，只得马上离去。谢端再三挽留不成。忽然天上刮风下雨，女子随风而去。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xie Duan memelihara siput besar dan kemudian menemukan makanan yang telah disiapkan setiap kali pulang.",
        "en": "Xie Duan keeps a large snail and later finds meals prepared whenever he returns home."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17060695-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q17060695-s1",
      "quote": "有一天他假装出门，躲在門缝後，看見水缸裡冒出来一個漂亮女子，為他煮好飯菜並把家裡打掃乾淨。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika mengintip, ia melihat seorang perempuan keluar dari tempat siput dan menyiapkan rumahnya.",
        "en": "When he watches secretly, he sees a woman emerge from the snail's container and attend to his house."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17060695-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q17060695-s1",
      "quote": "福建沿海一带的居民认为螺女是他们的守护神，能保佑他们出海时平安无事，所以他们相信螺女的传说，并且供奉螺女。螺女也是福州疍民的重要信仰之一。今福州仓山区有地名螺洲镇，闽侯县境内有地名螺江。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Warga pesisir Fujian memuja Gadis Siput sebagai pelindung keselamatan di laut.",
        "en": "People on the Fujian coast venerate the Snail Girl as a protector of safety at sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17060695-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q17060695-s1",
      "quote": "田螺姑娘的故事后来从福建省传到了江苏、浙江、上海、广东的沿海一带。另外，四川省射洪县的螺湖也存在着类似的神话传说。各地传说的版本内容都不大一样。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya menyebar ke beberapa wilayah pesisir dengan versi yang berbeda-beda.",
        "en": "The story spreads to several coastal regions in differing versions."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Longzhi adalah makhluk seperti rubah dengan sembilan kepala dan sembilan ekor. Cakarnya digambarkan menyerupai cakar harimau.",
      "en": "Longzhi is a foxlike creature with nine heads and nine tails. Its claws are described as resembling tiger claws.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17060952-c01",
        "wd-q17060952-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Suaranya menyerupai bayi. Longzhi digambarkan sebagai pemakan manusia. Bagian timur Shan Hai Jing menempatkan Longzhi pada Gunung Fuli. Gunung Fuli dalam uraian itu kaya emas dan giok pada bagian atas serta batu Zhen pada bagian bawah.",
      "en": "Its voice resembles a baby. Longzhi is described as a human eater. The eastern section of the Shan Hai Jing places Longzhi on Mount Fuli. Mount Fuli in that passage has abundant gold and jade above and Zhen stones below.",

        "wd-q17060952-c03",

      "quote": "又南五百里，曰鳧麗之山，其上多金玉，其下多箴石，有獸焉，其狀如狐，而九尾、九首、虎爪，名曰蠪姪，其音如嬰兒，是食人。",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Jiliang adalah kuda ajaib dalam legenda Tiongkok.",
    "en": "Jiliang is a fabulous horse in Chinese legend.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q22099589-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jiliang adalah kuda ajaib dalam legenda Tiongkok. Tubuhnya putih atau putih berpola, dengan mata keemasan.",
      "en": "Jiliang is a fabulous horse in Chinese legend. Its body is white or patterned white, with golden eyes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q22099589-c01",
        "wd-q22099589-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Surainya digambarkan merah. Shan Hai Jing menempatkan kuda ini dalam uraian negeri Quanfeng. Dalam teks itu, menunggang Jiliang dikatakan memberi umur seribu tahun. Dalam teks klasik, Jiliang memiliki tubuh putih, surai merah, dan mata seperti emas.",
      "en": "Its mane is described as red. The Shan Hai Jing places this horse in its description of the land of Quanfeng. In that text, riding Jiliang is said to grant a lifespan of a thousand years. In the classical text, Jiliang has a white body, red mane, and eyes like gold.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q22099589-c03",
        "wd-q22099589-c04",

      "source_id": "wd-q22099589-s1",
      "quote": "吉量是中国传说的奇馬，外型為純白或帶有斑紋的白馬,雙眼金黃,鬃毛赤紅,頸部似雞尾。別名「雞斯之乘」。也叫吉良，记载于《海内北经》。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jiliang adalah kuda ajaib dalam legenda Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Jiliang is a fabulous horse in Chinese legend."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q22099589-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q22099589-s1",
      "quote": "吉量是中国传说的奇馬，外型為純白或帶有斑紋的白馬,雙眼金黃,鬃毛赤紅,頸部似雞尾。別名「雞斯之乘」。也叫吉良，记载于《海内北经》。手游《山海镜花》中的镜灵也有吉良。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tubuhnya putih atau putih berpola, dengan mata keemasan.",
        "en": "Its body is white or patterned white, with golden eyes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q22099589-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q22099589-s1",
      "quote": "吉量是中国传说的奇馬，外型為純白或帶有斑紋的白馬,雙眼金黃,鬃毛赤紅,頸部似雞尾。別名「雞斯之乘」。也叫吉良，记载于《海内北经》。手游《山海镜花》中的镜灵也有吉良。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Surainya digambarkan merah.",
        "en": "Its mane is described as red."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q22099589-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q22099589-s1",
      "quote": "《山海经·海内北经》：“（犬封国）有文马，缟身朱鬣，目若黄金，名曰吉量，乘之寿千岁。”",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shan Hai Jing menempatkan kuda ini dalam uraian negeri Quanfeng.",
        "en": "The Shan Hai Jing places this horse in its description of the land of Quanfeng."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q22099589-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q22099589-s1",
      "quote": "《山海经·海内北经》：“（犬封国）有文马，缟身朱鬣，目若黄金，名曰吉量，乘之寿千岁。”",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam teks itu, menunggang Jiliang dikatakan memberi umur seribu tahun.",
        "en": "In that text, riding Jiliang is said to grant a lifespan of a thousand years."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q22099589-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q22099589-s2",
      "quote": "犬封國曰犬戎國，狀如犬。有一女子，方跪進柸食。有文馬，縞身朱鬛，目若黃金，名曰吉量，乘之壽千歲。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam teks klasik, Jiliang memiliki tubuh putih, surai merah, dan mata seperti emas.",

      }
    }

      "quote": "鰼鰼是中国神话里的一种异鱼，形状像鹊但十对翅膀，鳞片在羽毛末端，可以防火。",

      "source_id": "wd-q22099946-s2",
      "quote": "又北三百五十里，曰涿光之山。嚻水出焉，而西流注于河。其中多鰼鰼之魚，其狀如鵲而十翼，鱗皆在羽端，其音如鵲，可以禦火，食之不癉。其上多松柏，其下多椶枏，其獸多麢羊，其鳥多蕃。",

      "context": "historical-record",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Jiguang adalah hewan ajaib pembawa keberuntungan dalam mitologi Tiongkok.",
    "en": "Jiguang is an auspicious fabulous animal in Chinese mythology.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q9602528-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jiguang adalah hewan ajaib pembawa keberuntungan dalam mitologi Tiongkok. Salah satu uraian memberinya bentuk kuda putih berpola dengan surai merah yang lahir di rawa besar.",
      "en": "Jiguang is an auspicious fabulous animal in Chinese mythology. One description gives it the form of a patterned white horse with a red mane, born in a great marsh.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q9602528-c01",
        "wd-q9602528-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Jiguang kerap dicampur dengan Tenghuang, sementara bentuk rubah dan kuda berbeda menurut versinya. Ungkapan Jiguang pianyu memakai sehelai bulunya sebagai kiasan untuk peninggalan atau dokumen langka.",
      "en": "Jiguang is often confused with Tenghuang, while fox and horse forms differ between versions. The expression Jiguang pianyu uses a strand of its fur as a metaphor for rare relics or documents.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q9602528-c03",
        "wd-q9602528-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q9602528-s1",
      "url": "https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%90%89%E5%85%89",
      "title": "吉光 - 维基百科，自由的百科全书",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "zh",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-01"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q9602528-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q9602528-s1",
      "quote": "吉光，又稱吉良、澤馬，是中國神話傳說中的神獸，是吉祥的象徵。常與騰黃混淆在一起。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jiguang adalah hewan ajaib pembawa keberuntungan dalam mitologi Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Jiguang is an auspicious fabulous animal in Chinese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q9602528-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q9602528-s1",
      "quote": "吉光被認為是一種馬，出生在大澤中，身體是白的，帶著花紋，但是鬃毛是紅的。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu uraian memberinya bentuk kuda putih berpola dengan surai merah yang lahir di rawa besar.",
        "en": "One description gives it the form of a patterned white horse with a red mane, born in a great marsh."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q9602528-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q9602528-s1",
      "quote": "有人認為它就是騰黃。一種說法認為，騰黃長得像狐狸，是一種野獸，而吉光長得像馬；然而有些說法是反過來的。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jiguang kerap dicampur dengan Tenghuang, sementara bentuk rubah dan kuda berbeda menurut versinya.",
        "en": "Jiguang is often confused with Tenghuang, while fox and horse forms differ between versions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q9602528-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q9602528-s1",
      "quote": "因為吉光是極難得一見的神獸，因此民間有成語「吉光片羽」，以吉光的一絲獸毛，來形容某種極難得一見的文獻或是文物。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ungkapan Jiguang pianyu memakai sehelai bulunya sebagai kiasan untuk peninggalan atau dokumen langka.",
        "en": "The expression Jiguang pianyu uses a strand of its fur as a metaphor for rare relics or documents."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Xiao adalah nama yang digunakan untuk beberapa makhluk Tiongkok, termasuk kera berlengan panjang dan burung bersayap empat.",
    "en": "Xiao is a name used for several Chinese creatures, including a long armed ape and a four winged bird.",
    "claim_ids": [

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Xiao adalah nama yang digunakan untuk beberapa makhluk Tiongkok, termasuk kera berlengan panjang dan burung bersayap empat. Kata xiao mencakup makna gaduh atau bising.",
      "en": "Xiao is a name used for several Chinese creatures, including a long armed ape and a four winged bird. The word xiao includes meanings of noise and clamor.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "xiao-mythology-c01",
        "xiao-mythology-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Shan Hai Jing memakai nama Xiao untuk sungai, gunung, dan dua makhluk mitos. Salah satu Xiao menyerupai kera, berlengan panjang, dan mahir melempar benda. Xiao lain digambarkan sebagai burung dengan empat sayap, satu mata, dan ekor anjing. Komentar Guo Pu menyebut varian Jufu pada bagian yang membandingkan burung Xiao dengan Kuafu.",
      "en": "The Shan Hai Jing uses Xiao as a name for a river, a mountain, and two mythical creatures. One Xiao resembles an ape, has long arms, and is skilled at throwing things.       "id": "xiao-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "xiao-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "In Chinese mythology, the xiao is the name of several creatures, including the xiao (Chinese: 囂; pinyin: xiāo; Wade–Giles: hsiao1) \"a long-armed ape\" or \"a four-winged bird\" and shanxiao (Chinese: 山魈; pinyin: shānxiāo) \"mischievous, one-legged mountain spirit\". Furthermore, some Western sources misspell and misconstrue the older romanization hsiao as \"hsigo\" [sic] \"a flying monkey\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xiao adalah nama yang digunakan untuk beberapa makhluk Tiongkok, termasuk kera berlengan panjang dan burung bersayap empat.",
        "en": "Xiao is a name used for several Chinese creatures, including a long armed ape and a four winged bird."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xiao-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "xiao-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The Chinese word xiao (囂) means \"noise; clamor; hubbub; haughty; proud; arrogant\". During the Shang dynasty (c. 1600–1046 BCE), Xiao was both the name of a historical capital (near modern Zhengzhou in Henan province) during the era of King Zhong Ding (r.  c. 1421–1396 BCE), and the given name of King Geng Ding (r.  c. 1170–1147 BCE).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata xiao mencakup makna gaduh atau bising.",
        "en": "The word xiao includes meanings of noise and clamor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xiao-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "xiao-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The Shanhaijing \"Classic of Mountains and Seas\" uses Xiao (some editions write the graphic variant 嚻) as the name of a river (囂水; Xiaoshui), a mountain (北囂之山; Beixiao zhi shan), and two mythical creatures.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shan Hai Jing memakai nama Xiao untuk sungai, gunung, dan dua makhluk mitos.",
        "en": "The Shan Hai Jing uses Xiao as a name for a river, a mountain, and two mythical creatures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xiao-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "xiao-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Seventy leagues further west is a mountain called Mount Ewenext. … There is an animal on this mountain which looks like an ape, but it has longer arms and it is good at throwing things. Its name is the hubbub.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu Xiao menyerupai kera, berlengan panjang, dan mahir melempar benda.",
        "en": "One Xiao resembles an ape, has long arms, and is skilled at throwing things."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xiao-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "xiao-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Three hundred and fifty leagues further north is a mountain called Mount Bridgedrain. … There is a bird here which looks like the boastfather; it has four wings, one eye, and a dog's tail. Its name is the hubbub. It makes a noise like a magpie. If you eat it, it will cure a bellyache, and it is effective for indigestion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 11",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xiao lain digambarkan sebagai burung dengan empat sayap, satu mata, dan ekor anjing.",
        "en": "Another Xiao is described as a bird with four wings, one eye, and a dog's tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xiao-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "xiao-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Although this passage compares the Xiao bird with the humanoid Kuafu, the Shanhaijing commentary of Guo Pu (276–324) says an early textual version writes the Jufu (舉父), who is also described as yu \"monkey; ape\". The sub-commentary of Hao Yixing (郝懿行; 1757–1825) notes the association may be owing to the similar sounding names Kuafu and Jufu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 13",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Komentar Guo Pu menyebut varian Jufu pada bagian yang membandingkan burung Xiao dengan Kuafu.",
        "en": "Guo Pu's commentary records the variant Jufu in the passage comparing the Xiao bird with Kuafu."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Chisongzi adalah tokoh abadi dalam legenda Tiongkok, juga disebut Chisongzi dengan varian penulisan 赤诵子. Taiping Huanyu Ji mengaitkan kenaikannya ke langit dengan Gunung Chisong di Jinhua.",

        "chisongzi-c02"

      "id": "Liexian Zhuan menempatkan Chisongzi sebagai ahli hujan pada masa Shennong. Ia digambarkan tahan terhadap api dan dapat mengikuti hujan serta angin.       "quote": "赤松子又名赤诵子，号左圣南极南岳真人，又稱左仙太虚真人，秦汉传说中的上古仙人，與王子喬並列為長生的代表。",

    {
      "id": "chisongzi-c02",
      "source_id": "chisongzi-s1",
      "quote": "《太平寰宇记》说赤松子是在金华的赤松山（金华山），以火自焚尸解升天，其处为赤松涧，故山上有赤松祠。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taiping Huanyu Ji mengaitkan kenaikannya ke langit dengan Gunung Chisong di Jinhua.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chisongzi-c03",
      "source_id": "chisongzi-s1",
      "quote": "刘向的《列仙传》记载赤松子本是神农时人，为雨师，他服食水玉，把它教给神农，能够在烈火中任火烧烤。赤松子常常去昆仑山上，在西王母的石室之中歇息，随风雨自由上下。炎帝的小女儿曾跟随他，亦成仙飞升而去。又据《韩诗外传》载，赤松子曾为帝喾之师。",

      "id": "chisongzi-c04",
      "source_id": "chisongzi-s1",
      "quote": "刘向的《列仙传》记载赤松子本是神农时人，为雨师，他服食水玉，把它教给神农，能够在烈火中任火烧烤。赤松子常常去昆仑山上，在西王母的石室之中歇息，随风雨自由上下。炎帝的小女儿曾跟随他，亦成仙飞升而去。又据《韩诗外传》载，赤松子曾为帝喾之师。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",

      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "chisongzi-c05",

      "quote": "刘向的《列仙传》记载赤松子本是神农时人，为雨师，他服食水玉，把它教给神农，能够在烈火中任火烧烤。赤松子常常去昆仑山上，在西王母的石室之中歇息，随风雨自由上下。炎帝的小女儿曾跟随他，亦成仙飞升而去。又据《韩诗外传》载，赤松子曾为帝喾之师。",

      "id": "chisongzi-c06",
      "source_id": "chisongzi-s1",
      "quote": "关于赤松子服水玉一事，文献中记载中多有不同。《山海经·南山经》注中说赤松子所服食的水玉就是水精（水晶），干宝的《搜神记》则称为冰玉散，葛洪的《抱朴子》则说赤松子服食的是神丹，并有赤松子丹法传世。相传也是葛洪所著的《神仙传》中则称黃初平（皇初平）为赤松子，服松腊茯苓。《丹台录》则称赤松子为昆林仙伯，辖牿南岳山，可化玉为水而服。",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hairen adalah manusia atau makhluk menyerupai manusia penghuni laut dalam khazanah Tiongkok, disebut kaijin dalam sumber Jepang.",
    "en": "Hairen are sea dwelling humans or humanoids in Chinese lore, called kaijin in Japanese sources.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hairen-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hairen adalah manusia atau makhluk menyerupai manusia penghuni laut dalam khazanah Tiongkok, disebut kaijin dalam sumber Jepang. Catatan berbahasa Tionghoa yang ditulis orang Eropa menggambarkan tangan dan kaki berselaput.",
      "en": "Hairen are sea dwelling humans or humanoids in Chinese lore, called kaijin in Japanese sources. Chinese language accounts written by Europeans describe webbed hands and feet.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hairen-c01",
        "hairen-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Catatan Edo seperti Yamato Honzō mengulang kisah makhluk dengan kulit menjuntai seperti celana hakama. Giulio Aleni dan Ferdinand Verbiest menerbitkan keterangan hairen dalam karya berbahasa Tionghoa. Salah satu kisah menyebut makhluk yang ditangkap dan diberikan kepada raja, lalu dilepas karena tidak dapat dijinakkan. Kisah perempuan hairen dihubungkan dengan legenda Mermaid of Edam dari Belanda pada 1403.",
      "en": "Edo accounts such as Yamato Honzō repeat the description of a creature with skin hanging like hakama trousers. Giulio Aleni and Ferdinand Verbiest publish descriptions of hairen in Chinese language works. One story describes a creature captured and presented to a king, then released because it could not be tamed. The account of a female hairen is connected with the Dutch Mermaid of Edam legend of 1403.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hairen-c03",
        "hairen-c04",
        "hairen-c05",
        "hairen-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "hairen-c01",
      "source_id": "hairen-s1",
      "quote": "A hairen (海人, \"sea-human\") is a sea-dwelling human or humanoid in Chinese lore, also called  kaijin (海人) by Japanese sources.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hairen adalah manusia atau makhluk menyerupai manusia penghuni laut dalam khazanah Tiongkok, disebut kaijin dalam sumber Jepang.",
        "en": "Hairen are sea dwelling humans or humanoids in Chinese lore, called kaijin in Japanese sources."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hairen-c02",
      "source_id": "hairen-s1",
      "quote": "According to a Chinese texts which were actually authored by Europeans, the hairen was human-like overall but possessed webbed feet and hands, and one was captured in Holland, who had loose drooping skin on her whole body, almost like she wore a piece of garment (specifically a paofu or long robe).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan berbahasa Tionghoa yang ditulis orang Eropa menggambarkan tangan dan kaki berselaput.",
        "en": "Chinese language accounts written by Europeans describe webbed hands and feet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hairen-c03",
      "source_id": "hairen-s1",
      "quote": "The account of the \"sea-humans\" also recurred in Japanese sources dating to the Edo Period, such as Kaibara Ekken's Yamato honzō, which describe the creature as having skin that hangs down to the ground, like a pair of hakama trousers being worn.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan Edo seperti Yamato Honzō mengulang kisah makhluk dengan kulit menjuntai seperti celana hakama.",
        "en": "Edo accounts such as Yamato Honzō repeat the description of a creature with skin hanging like hakama trousers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hairen-c04",
      "source_id": "hairen-s1",
      "quote": "Books written by Europeans and published in the Chinese language during the Ming and Qing dynasties mention the hairen. Italian Jesuit Giulio Aleni (Chinese name: Airulüe 艾儒略) wrote in his Zhifang waiji (simplified Chinese: 职方外纪; traditional Chinese: 職方外紀, \"Records of Regions beyond the Jurisdiction of the Imperial Geographer\", 1623) that there are two types of hairen.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Giulio Aleni dan Ferdinand Verbiest menerbitkan keterangan hairen dalam karya berbahasa Tionghoa.",
        "en": "Giulio Aleni and Ferdinand Verbiest publish descriptions of hairen in Chinese language works."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hairen-c05",
      "source_id": "hairen-s1",
      "quote": "The first type was overall human-like, with both beards and eyebrows, but having hands and feet which were webbed, like those of wild ducks. An individual was captured in the Western (European) sea, and presented to a certain king, but ate nothing, and having failed to tame it, the king was forced to release it back to sea, and the liberated creature clapped its hands and laughed loudly.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu kisah menyebut makhluk yang ditangkap dan diberikan kepada raja, lalu dilepas karena tidak dapat dijinakkan.",
        "en": "One story describes a creature captured and presented to a king, then released because it could not be tamed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hairen-c06",
      "source_id": "hairen-s1",
      "quote": "This female hairen has been determined to be the one captured in Holland in 1403 (the so-called Mermaid of Edam), described in a number of 17th century European works. For example, this \"marine woman\" (Latin: mulier quaedam marina) or \"siren\" was caught in 1403 by fishermen, having been cast into the sea by storm according to Petrus Scriverius (d.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah perempuan hairen dihubungkan dengan legenda Mermaid of Edam dari Belanda pada 1403.",
        "en": "The account of a female hairen is connected with the Dutch Mermaid of Edam legend of 1403."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

      "en": "The Kanas Lake Monster is a creature reportedly living in Kanas Lake, Xinjiang; its existence remains unconfirmed. Its reported form is fishlike, and one hypothesis connects it with large taimen.",

Artikel menyebut laporan seorang penggembala Mongol pada 1931 sebagai catatan penampakan awal. Li Sizhong menafsirkan bayangan merah sebagai kemungkinan kawanan taimen, bukan satu hewan raksasa. Penyelidikan Ren Mulian pada 1988 menyimpulkan bahwa taimen setempat tidak melebihi empat meter.",
      "en": "Local Tuvan tradition describes water monsters swallowing cattle and horses on the shore. The article gives a Mongolian herder's report in 1931 as an early recorded sighting.     {
      "id": "kanas-lake-monster-c01",
      "source_id": "kanas-lake-monster-s1",
      "quote": "喀纳斯湖水怪（英语：Kanas Lake Monster），是据传生活在中国新疆维吾尔自治区喀纳斯湖中的不明生物。与世界各地的其它湖怪不同，喀纳斯湖水怪并非传说的爬虫类或哺乳类，而类似于鱼类的生物。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Monster Danau Kanas adalah makhluk yang dilaporkan hidup di Danau Kanas, Xinjiang; keberadaannya belum dipastikan.",
        "en": "The Kanas Lake Monster is a creature reportedly living in Kanas Lake, Xinjiang; its existence remains unconfirmed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanas-lake-monster-c02",
      "source_id": "kanas-lake-monster-s1",
      "quote": "喀纳斯湖水怪（英语：Kanas Lake Monster），是据传生活在中国新疆维吾尔自治区喀纳斯湖中的不明生物。与世界各地的其它湖怪不同，喀纳斯湖水怪并非传说的爬虫类或哺乳类，而类似于鱼类的生物。在常见的理论中喀纳斯湖水怪被认为可能是巨型哲罗鲑。曾在新疆北部调查过鱼类、主持编写《新疆鱼类志》（1979 年版）的鱼类学家李思忠在与方芳 等考察过喀纳斯湖之后认为，人们传言所见的“大红鱼”为哲罗鲑鱼群，而非其个体。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk yang dilaporkan menyerupai ikan, dan salah satu hipotesis menghubungkannya dengan taimen berukuran besar.",
        "en": "Its reported form is fishlike, and one hypothesis connects it with large taimen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanas-lake-monster-c03",
      "source_id": "kanas-lake-monster-s1",
      "quote": "据当地的图瓦人称，在许久以前，当地就有巨型水怪吞噬岸边牛马的传说。到了1931年，首次出现目击记录，据说当地一位蒙古牧民在湖边目击到了几十个类似于鱼类的巨型水怪。 据传闻，当地图瓦人曾组织过猎捕湖怪的行动，但均以失败告终。最近一次是在80年代，当地两名图瓦族猎人试图捕捉他们，结果永远消失在水怪经常出没的地方。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi masyarakat Tuvan setempat menyebut monster air yang menelan sapi dan kuda di tepi danau.",
        "en": "Local Tuvan tradition describes water monsters swallowing cattle and horses on the shore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanas-lake-monster-c04",
      "source_id": "kanas-lake-monster-s1",
      "quote": "据当地的图瓦人称，在许久以前，当地就有巨型水怪吞噬岸边牛马的传说。到了1931年，首次出现目击记录，据说当地一位蒙古牧民在湖边目击到了几十个类似于鱼类的巨型水怪。 据传闻，当地图瓦人曾组织过猎捕湖怪的行动，但均以失败告终。最近一次是在80年代，当地两名图瓦族猎人试图捕捉他们，结果永远消失在水怪经常出没的地方。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel menyebut laporan seorang penggembala Mongol pada 1931 sebagai catatan penampakan awal.",
        "en": "The article gives a Mongolian herder's report in 1931 as an early recorded sighting."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanas-lake-monster-c05",
      "source_id": "kanas-lake-monster-s1",
      "quote": "1988年7月，中国科学院动物研究所鱼类学家李思忠与方芳等前往喀纳斯湖考察，曾在观鱼亭用望眼镜观测到“有时相合，有时分离，很模糊”的大红鱼暗影，认为可能为哲罗鲑鱼群；鱼个体长度不可能超过4—5米。 1988年7月12日，中国水产科学研究院教授任慕莲带领一支考察队来到了喀纳斯湖进行了为期两个月考察，任慕莲他们的结论是这里的哲罗鲑的长度不会超过四米。 此后，目击事件似乎也随着他们考察结束而逐渐冷淡下来。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Li Sizhong menafsirkan bayangan merah sebagai kemungkinan kawanan taimen, bukan satu hewan raksasa.",
        "en": "Li Sizhong interprets the red shadows as possibly a shoal of taimen rather than one giant animal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanas-lake-monster-c06",
      "source_id": "kanas-lake-monster-s1",
      "quote": "1988年7月，中国科学院动物研究所鱼类学家李思忠与方芳等前往喀纳斯湖考察，曾在观鱼亭用望眼镜观测到“有时相合，有时分离，很模糊”的大红鱼暗影，认为可能为哲罗鲑鱼群；鱼个体长度不可能超过4—5米。 1988年7月12日，中国水产科学研究院教授任慕莲带领一支考察队来到了喀纳斯湖进行了为期两个月考察，任慕莲他们的结论是这里的哲罗鲑的长度不会超过四米。 此后，目击事件似乎也随着他们考察结束而逐渐冷淡下来。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penyelidikan Ren Mulian pada 1988 menyimpulkan bahwa taimen setempat tidak melebihi empat meter.",
        "en": "Ren Mulian's investigation in 1988 concludes that local taimen do not exceed four metres."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Klu adalah dewa naga atau roh air dalam tradisi Tibet. Dalam Bon, sebagian membawa panen dan kebahagiaan, sedangkan sebagian membawa penyakit dan bencana.",
      "en": "Klu are dragon deities or water spirits in Tibetan tradition. In Bon, some bring harvests and happiness while others bring disease and disaster.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "klu-c01",
        "klu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bentuk awal Klu tidak tetap dan dapat berupa beragam hewan air. Teks Bon menggambarkan bentuk gabungan dengan tubuh manusia, berbagai kepala hewan, dan ekor ular atau ikan. Tradisi Bon membagi kelompok Klu ke arah timur, selatan, barat, utara, dan tengah. Sesudah Buddhisme berkembang di Tibet, Klu dibandingkan atau disamakan dengan naga India.",
      "en": "Early Klu forms are variable and can include different aquatic animals. Bon texts describe composite forms with human bodies, various animal heads, and snake or fish tails. Bon tradition divides Klu groups among east, south, west, north, and centre. After Buddhism develops in Tibet, Klu are compared or equated with Indian nagas.",

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "klu-s1",
      "url": "https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E9%B2%81_(%E9%BE%99)",
      "title": "鲁 (龙) - 维基百科，自由的百科全书",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "zh",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-01"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "klu-c01",
      "source_id": "klu-s1",
      "quote": "鲁（藏語：ཀླུ，威利转写：klu，THL：lü），或半音半意译云鲁龙，是藏族文化中的龙神。在苯教传说中，鲁龙居住在五個方向的山林池澤中，其中有善類，帶來豐收和幸福，也有惡類，帶來疾疫和災難；魯龙是重要的水界神靈，传说作为天神之子的吐蕃王赞普，最初皆与龙女或神女婚配；佛教傳入藏地後，魯龙和印度文化中的那伽神龍（蛇龍類神獸）等同起來。考古認為魯龙文化的起源非常久遠，可能是漢藏民族的共同信仰，在纳西、彝、傈僳等其他民族中都有流传，和汉地的龙文化也可能同源。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Klu adalah dewa naga atau roh air dalam tradisi Tibet.",
        "en": "Klu are dragon deities or water spirits in Tibetan tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klu-c02",
      "source_id": "klu-s1",
      "quote": "鲁（藏語：ཀླུ，威利转写：klu，THL：lü），或半音半意译云鲁龙，是藏族文化中的龙神。在苯教传说中，鲁龙居住在五個方向的山林池澤中，其中有善類，帶來豐收和幸福，也有惡類，帶來疾疫和災難；魯龙是重要的水界神靈，传说作为天神之子的吐蕃王赞普，最初皆与龙女或神女婚配；佛教傳入藏地後，魯龙和印度文化中的那伽神龍（蛇龍類神獸）等同起來。考古認為魯龙文化的起源非常久遠，可能是漢藏民族的共同信仰，在纳西、彝、傈僳等其他民族中都有流传，和汉地的龙文化也可能同源。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Bon, sebagian membawa panen dan kebahagiaan, sedangkan sebagian membawa penyakit dan bencana.",
        "en": "In Bon, some bring harvests and happiness while others bring disease and disaster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klu-c03",
      "source_id": "klu-s1",
      "quote": "根據對遺跡、壁畫和史料記載等的考古研究，推断“魯”龍神崇拜起源於吐蕃以前的原始社會。早期的鲁龙形象复杂多变，可以附身或化身爲水生動物，沒有固定的形象，甚至可以泛指魚、蝦、蛙、蛇、蝎、蟹等。牠們能帶來420多種疾病，如瘟疫、梅毒、傷寒、天花、麻風等，藏人稱之為“龍病”。在近代的川西南藏区仍流传着“亵渎了水中的神灵，身上就会起水泡、长脓疮”的说法，是“龙病”观点的遗留。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk awal Klu tidak tetap dan dapat berupa beragam hewan air.",
        "en": "Early Klu forms are variable and can include different aquatic animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klu-c04",
      "source_id": "klu-s1",
      "quote": "在苯教经过辛饶米沃祖师的改革而进入恰苯时期後，鲁龙被作为重要的神祇加以供奉，其形象演變為多種動物的綜合。苯教《大品黑白花濟龍經》（《十万龙经》）記載，龍神有人身、馬頭，或蛇頭、虎頭、熊頭、鼠頭、羊頭、牛頭、豹頭、豕頭、鹿頭、狼頭、孔雀頭等等不同形象，且都有蛇尾或魚尾。這與漢地龍形象的演變頗有相近之處，即“九擬”理論——角似鹿（最初漢地龍沒有角）、頭似駝、眼似兔、項似蛇、腹似蜃、鱗似魚、爪似鷹、掌似虎、身如牛。此時期，魯龍的故事也在原始傳說基礎上豐富而具體起來。如苯教经典《花黑白十万龙经》（简称《十万龙经》，也稱《十二萬龍經》）中寫道，魯龍是世界誕生之母，故稱“龍母”，它們居住在水域中，或山林中，也說住在海底龍宮，守护着无数财宝。藏学家海爾姆特·霍夫曼在《西藏的宗教》一书中写道：“这些龙的最初的住所是河和湖，甚至是些井；他们在水底有家，守卫着秘密的财富。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Bon menggambarkan bentuk gabungan dengan tubuh manusia, berbagai kepala hewan, dan ekor ular atau ikan.",
        "en": "Bon texts describe composite forms with human bodies, various animal heads, and snake or fish tails."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klu-c05",
      "source_id": "klu-s1",
      "quote": "這一時期，魯龍有了方位的概念，和道教的五方龍王類似，苯教也認為在東南西北中五個方位各有龍神部族鎮守，東部嘉讓（甲仁），南部解讓（吉尔让），西部莽讓（芒仁），北部章賽讓（壮色仁），中部得巴讓（毒巴仁）。其中嘉讓是善神，給人類帶來幸福，莽讓是兇神，帶來瘟疫、疾病、災難，其他三類魯龍則性無定，高興時帶來幸福，氣憤時帶來禍亂，這就給“龍病”的來源賦予了新的解釋。同時，苯教還認為有一種叫“恰布”（恰柏）的龍神，管理著世間的雨、雪、雷電等的施降；“僧波”（松崩）龙神，主管各种“龙病”；“却热”（曲日）、“俄本”龙神，则主管饑饉、爭戰；還有“噶波”、“寧噶”（年噶）龍神主管人類的正直心，等等。藏學家丹珠昂奔教授認為，魯龍神的“善”的品質在苯教教義發展中得到提升，是與吐蕃王族的神化背景相聯繫的。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Bon membagi kelompok Klu ke arah timur, selatan, barat, utara, dan tengah.",
        "en": "Bon tradition divides Klu groups among east, south, west, north, and centre."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klu-c06",
      "source_id": "klu-s1",
      "quote": "那伽传入汉地後，因居住在水域、帶來降雨的特徵和本土的龙类同，故将那伽翻译爲神龙、龙；藏地与之类似，亦将鲁龙比附为那伽。当然，藏族、汉族、印度的十二生肖明显同源，而汉藏生肖中的龙与印度的那伽相对应，也有人认为这是这一类精怪生物或图腾在不同文化系统中的不同表现，故本身或许具有同源性。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sesudah Buddhisme berkembang di Tibet, Klu dibandingkan atau disamakan dengan naga India.",
        "en": "After Buddhism develops in Tibet, Klu are compared or equated with Indian nagas."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

      "en": "The Prespa Lake Monster is a creature reported from Lake Prespa in the Balkans; its identity remains unproven. Reported descriptions present a long necked animal resembling the Loch Ness Monster.",

      "id": "Artikel mencatat laporan penampakan pada 2012 dan rekaman video lain pada 2017. Penyelidikan setelah laporan tersebut disebut tidak menemukan monster itu. Hipotesis ikan lele dan dugaan rekayasa video merupakan penafsiran yang berbeda, bukan identifikasi yang telah pasti.",
      "en": "The article records a reported sighting in 2012 and another video in 2017. Investigations following the reports are said to have found no monster. A catfish hypothesis and suspicions of a staged video are differing interpretations rather than a confirmed identification.",

      "id": "prespa-lake-monster-c01",
      "source_id": "prespa-lake-monster-s1",
      "quote": "普雷斯帕湖怪物（英文：Prespa Lake Monster）是指出没于欧洲巴尔干半岛普雷斯帕湖的湖怪。普雷斯帕湖怪物在当地一直有所传闻，其被描述为类似长颈尼斯湖怪的动物。2012年，一对夫妇在阿尔巴尼亚尼维察（Nivica）附近希腊湖区目击湖怪出没。英国广播公司对普雷斯帕湖展开调查，但没有收获。2017年，阿尔巴尼亚普雷托村（Pretor）再度有目击者发现长颈湖怪，并拍下一段视频。之后退休地理学教授Sima Jonoski与记者们一同展开调查，但没有发现湖怪踪迹。有理论认为普雷斯帕湖怪物其实是湖中的鲶鱼，但反对者认为鲶鱼与影像中的湖怪形象不符。神秘动物学研究者安德鲁·麦格拉思（Andrew McGrath）对2017年12月流出的湖怪影像真实性表示怀疑，认为这可能是一场骗局。",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Qingniao adalah burung biru atau hijau dalam mitologi Tiongkok. Burung ini melayani atau menjadi utusan Xi Wangmu, Ratu Ibu dari Barat.",
      "en": "Qingniao are blue or green birds in Chinese mythology. These birds serve Xi Wangmu, the Queen Mother of the West, or act as her messengers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "qingniao-c01",
        "qingniao-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagian versi memberi utusan Qingniao tiga kaki. Versi lain menyebut seekor burung berkaki satu yang mengambil makanan untuk Xi Wangmu. Ada pula versi tiga burung hijau yang membawakan makanannya. Qingniao menjadi motif kisah Xi Wangmu dan surga baratnya yang umumnya ditempatkan di Kunlun.",
      "en": "Some versions give the messenger Qingniao three legs. Other versions describe a single one legged bird fetching food for Xi Wangmu. There is also a version in which three green birds bring her food. Qingniao form a motif in accounts of Xi Wangmu and her western paradise, generally placed on Kunlun.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "qingniao-c03",
        "qingniao-c04",
        "qingniao-c05",
        "qingniao-c06"
      ]
    }

'Blue (or Green) Bird (or birds)') were blue or green birds which appear in Chinese mythology, popular stories, poetry, and religion (the Chinese are somewhat ambiguous in regard to English color vocabulary, and the word qing may and has been translated as \"blue\" or \"green\", or even \"black\").",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Qingniao adalah burung biru atau hijau dalam mitologi Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Qingniao are blue or green birds in Chinese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "qingniao-c02",
      "source_id": "qingniao-s1",
      "quote": "Qingniao is especially regarded as the messengers or as otherwise serving the Queen Mother of the West Xi Wangmu.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Burung ini melayani atau menjadi utusan Xi Wangmu, Ratu Ibu dari Barat.",

      }
    },

      "id": "Ro-langs biasanya dibangkitkan oleh roh gdon atau ahli sihir. Makhluk ini tidak dapat berbicara atau membungkuk dan bergerak dengan sendi kaku.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Ro-langs are usually raised by a gdon spirit or a sorcerer."

    },
    {
      "id": "ro-langs-c04",
      "source_id": "ro-langs-s1",
      "quote": "A ro-langs cannot speak or bend over, it signals its victims by wagging its tongue back and forth.  They can not bend any joints, which makes them walk with a stiff-armed lurch.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk ini tidak dapat berbicara atau membungkuk dan bergerak dengan sendi kaku.",
        "en": "The creature cannot speak or bend over and moves with stiff joints."
      }

      "id": "ro-langs-c05",
      "source_id": "ro-langs-s1",
      "quote": "The tantric types of ro-langs are raised from the dead through a ritual for personal reasons, such as to serve a necromancer and satisfy his lust for the power of the occult.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The tantric type is raised through ritual for the purposes of the person performing it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ro-langs-c06",
      "source_id": "ro-langs-s1",
      "quote": "Demonic ro-langs are created by an evil spirit with the goal of contaminating other humans; they are independent, and do not serve a master. They are created by either a gdon spirit that has broken its oath and become evil, or a bgegs spirit, which is already evil.  Once the consciousness has left the body, the evil spirit enters the body before it is buried.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jenis demonik digambarkan mandiri dan berusaha mencemari manusia lain.",

      "en": "Schytinglitto is an evil spirit associated with illness in Siraya legends from Taiwan. The spirit is described as having no physical form.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "schytinglitto-c01",
        "schytinglitto-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Orang yang dianggap diganggunya memanggil pendeta perempuan Inibs untuk mengusirnya. Ritual pengusiran menggunakan persembahan, pisau, dan arak serta bantuan pemuda. Roh diarahkan ke aliran air atau hutan, dan bambu ditegakkan untuk menghalangi kepulangannya. Jika penyakit berlanjut, pendeta menggali lubang dan memperlihatkan rambut yang diklaim berasal dari roh.",
      "en": "People thought to be troubled by it call an Inibs priestess to expel it. The expulsion ritual uses offerings, a knife, and liquor, with the help of young men.     "id": "Laba-laba Berkaki Tujuh adalah makhluk pengganggu tidur dalam cerita rakyat Taiwan.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Laba-laba Berkaki Tujuh adalah makhluk pengganggu tidur dalam cerita rakyat Taiwan. Masyarakat menganggap laba-laba yang lahir dengan tujuh kaki sebagai roh jahat.",
      "en": "Seven legged spiders are sleep disturbing creatures in Taiwanese folklore. Spiders born with seven legs are regarded as evil spirits.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "seven-legs-spiders-c01",
        "seven-legs-spiders-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Langkahnya di tubuh orang tidur dihubungkan dengan pola tujuh bintang dan kelumpuhan tidur. Makhluk ini juga dikatakan menghasilkan suara aneh dekat telinga. Meletakkan secangkir air di dekat kepala tempat tidur disebut sebagai cara mengatasinya dalam tradisi itu. Sebuah kisah menyebut seorang pemuda menemukan laba-laba tersebut mengetuk kaki dan bersuara ketika menyalakan lilin.",
      "en": "Its steps across a sleeper are connected with a seven star pattern and sleep paralysis. The creature is also said to make unusual sounds near the ears.   "short_description": {
    "id": "Si adalah hewan bertanduk satu yang menyerupai sapi dalam teks kuno Tiongkok; identifikasi zoologisnya memerlukan kehati-hatian.",
    "en": "Si is a single horned oxlike animal in ancient Chinese texts; its zoological identification requires care.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "si-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Si adalah hewan bertanduk satu yang menyerupai sapi dalam teks kuno Tiongkok; identifikasi zoologisnya memerlukan kehati-hatian. Teks seperti Analects dan Zhuangzi menyebut Si sebagai contoh hewan buas bersama harimau dan jiao.",
      "en": "Si is a single horned oxlike animal in ancient Chinese texts; its zoological identification requires care. Texts such as the Analects and Zhuangzi mention Si as a fierce beast alongside tigers and jiao.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "si-c01",
        "si-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Komentar Guo Pu pada Erya menggambarkannya bertanduk satu dan berwarna biru gelap. Erya membandingkan Si dengan sapi dan xi dengan babi. Sejumlah teks menghubungkan Si dengan perburuan penguasa Chu. Zhou Li dan teks lain menyebut kulit Si sebagai bahan untuk baju zirah.",
      "en": "Guo Pu's Erya commentary describes it as single horned and dark blue. The Erya compares Si with an ox and xi with a pig. Several texts associate Si with the hunts of Chu rulers. The Zhou Li and other texts mention Si hides as material for armour.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "si-c03",
        "si-c04",
        "si-c05",
        "si-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
